5                                                                                                5
                    This paper develops Neural Bellman Operators for policy evaluation
6                                                                                                6
                 and feasible improvement in controlled economies. Centered continuation
7                                                                                                7
                 errors link economic transport to full-policy loss. A constructive neural
8                backend retains the feasible actions attaining its labels and supports im-      8

9                plementation under acquired states and endogenous capacity. We give an          9

10               exact continuous-state compiler that preserves the original policy, includ-     10
                 ing ties, while removing a redundant state-cover factor from its evaluation
11                                                                                               11
                 work. The construction combines classical distance transforms with witness-
12                                                                                               12
                 preserving off-grid evaluation and numerical certification. A priced obser-
13               vation technology determines precision from a certified economic account.       13

14               Direct constrained-policy comparisons distinguish signed cost differences       14

15               from the ability to recoup a controller replacement fee. Matched services in-   15
                 clude uniform and error-driven conventional methods, failed refinements,
16                                                                                               16
                 and three- and four-dimensional nonlinear stress cases. The results separate
17                                                                                               17
                 representation savings, complete work, and economic decisions without
18               attributing a native-envelope capability exclusively to neural encoding.        18

19               Broader applications retain their own comparison conditions.                    19

20                                                                                               20

21                  K EYWORDS : Dynamic programming, Neural approximation, Policy cer-           21
                 tification, Recursive utility, Computational economics.

                                      1. INTRODUCTION                                         12

13       An economic decision uses a continuation value because its consequences extend       13

14   beyond the current period. A learned continuation is useful only to the extent           14

15   that the decisions made with it are accurate. Prediction loss, a Bellman residual        15

16   at sampled states, and the value of the policy actually implemented are different        16

17   objects. Their distinction is particularly important when a counterfactual changes       17

18   future policies or when a recursive preference evaluates the continuation generated      18

19   by those policies rather than the optimum.                                               19

20       This paper develops Neural Bellman Operators (NBOs) as a policy-evaluation and       20

21   feasible-improvement method. An operator takes economic primitives, a current            21

22   policy, and computational resources as inputs. It returns a continuation, a feasible     22

23   policy, and an economic error account, or it records that the requested account was      23

24   not obtained within its cap. The continuation is evaluated against its own fitted        24

25   future. Current actions, policy-specific future values, numerical execution, and the     25

26   cost of verification enter the same account. The construction is not a proposal to       26

27   rename an arbitrary neural predictor as a Bellman operator.                              27

28       There are four parts to the argument. First, only action-dependent continuation      28

29   errors affect a current decision. Their oscillation, rather than their absolute level,   29

30   controls loss. A transport bound identifies when an anchor continuation remains          30

31   valid after the future economy changes. These results connect reuse and refresh          31

32   decisions to economic tolerances. They also preserve the original formulation in         32
                                 NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                 3

1    controlled diffusions, recursive preferences, endogenous preferences, and games,          1

2    subject to the distinct hypotheses of those applications.                                 2

3      Second, a signed residual certificate controls a complete returned policy. Its upper    3

4    bound contains the widths of the residual enclosures, the suboptimality of the            4

5    selected actions, and the terminal enclosure. It is invariant to additive date-specific   5

6    shifts of the fitted continuation. This leads to a useful separation: a policy can be     6

7    accurate even though a coarse verifier does not establish its accuracy. We prove          7

8    that residual verification can be refined while retaining the original network, the       8

9    original deployed actor, and its original allowance. The resulting bound measures         9

10   improved knowledge of the same policy, not a policy improvement disguised as              10

11   additional verification.                                                                  11

12     Third, certification of each policy relative to the optimum does not rank two           12

13   policies. We construct their cost difference directly. Under expected-cost preferences,   13

14   an own-continuation Bellman telescoping identity produces an unbiased policy-cost         14

15   score with a support width governed by the signed certificate. Coupling innovations       15

16   across policies and enclosing every simulated path gives simultaneous finite-sample       16

17   intervals for the difference in the original continuous-innovation economy. The           17

18   statistical assertion is conditional on frozen policies and the stated independent        18

19   simulation model; outward arithmetic and unresolved action-cell boundaries have           19

20   their own explicit treatment.                                                             20

21     Fourth, a deterministic neural construction connects primitive moduli to a com- 21
22   plete policy tolerance. A minimum of Lipschitz cones, realized exactly by ReLU            22

23   gates, fits each date’s own-future Bellman labels without inflating its Lipschitz         23

24   bound when those labels are inexact. The state, action, and arithmetic budgets are        24

25   specified before construction. This yields finite termination for a defined neural        25

26   backend rather than a claim about arbitrary nonlinear training. A new full matched        26

27   catalogue executes the same resolution ladder for this backend and a conventional         27

28   spline, including every unsuccessful rung and three isolated timing repetitions.          28

29     The action that attains a Bellman label supplies additional information. We retain      29

30   its index through the ReLU minimum circuit and deploy that action when its                30

31   cone is selected. A one-sided policy sandwich then avoids charging the separate           31

32   actor and critic accounts twice. Both the nearest-node neural ablation and the            32
     4

1    spline receive a strengthened certificate. Their complete matched frontiers isolate      1

2    certificate sharpness from first-target work and actual policy cost.                     2

3        A feasible policy must also survive the information and arithmetic used to imple- 3
4    ment it. We give a uniform acquisition-aware theorem that permits discontinuous          4

5    witness selection, uncertain measured states and capacity-dependent repair. A cou- 5
6    pled two-state investment economy realizes all covering, integration and repair op- 6
7    erations explicitly, with a conventional fitted-value comparator. The original scalar    7

8    witness policies receive direct expected-cost comparisons at every common first          8

9    crossing, under a tolerance and simultaneous error account fixed before execution. 9
10   This establishes proximity within the declared decision band, not a signed neural        10

11   ranking. A catalogue-choice theorem makes the result usable when implementation          11

12   charges differ. The complete resource curves and an identical native-envelope con- 12
13   trol isolate what the construction, the certificate and the neural representation each   13

14   contribute.                                                                              14

15       Three further results connect this construction to computation and economic          15

16   implementation. First, a literal cone network repeats a scan over every state label      16

17   whenever a Bellman query evaluates its future continuation. On a tensor cover, we        17

18   compile this network once and answer an arbitrary continuous-state query from the        18

19   cell’s corner data. The compiler carries the original action witness and the original    19

20   tie rule. Consequently the improvement in evaluation work does not come from             20

21   changing the policy being evaluated. Classical separable distance transforms supply      21

22   the nodal computation; the additional policy argument establishes the continuous         22

23   off-grid identity and feasible witness realization. In the two-state construction this   23

24   removes the redundant factor that makes dense cone evaluation scale as N 6 rather        24

25   than N 4 . It does not remove the state cover or give a capability unavailable to the    25

26   identical native envelope.                                                               26

27       Second, we make observation accuracy a priced economic input. A finite-bit           27

28   sensor reports a state cell. Robust repair selects an action feasible throughout that    28

29   cell. The policy theorem then gives an augmented loss account consisting of the full- 29
30   information construction bound, an acquisition term decreasing geometrically in the      30

31   number of bits, and a linear information charge. Exact neighboring-bit comparisons       31

32   identify the precision minimizing this guarantee. This is an information-design          32
                                 NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                   5

1    result for the stated technology, not an assertion that minimizing a bound also             1

2    minimizes the unknown actual loss.                                                          2

3      Third, we evaluate actual constrained-policy cost differences, independently of           3

4    their all-state regret certificates. The two installed controllers face common continu- 4
5    ous innovations and the same observation technology. A prospective replacement              5

6    charge turns simultaneous expected-cost intervals into a decision about whether             6

7    either replacement recoups its cost. That question differs both from the sign of the        7

8    policy difference and from exact equality. A complete matched construction study,           8

9    an error-driven conventional comparator, and higher-dimensional nonlinear stress            9

10   executions accompany the decision analysis. We charge compilation, adaptive pilots,         10

11   unsuccessful resolutions, repair and output, and report the joint policy-comparison         11

12   service separately. These distinctions permit a positive computational contribution         12

13   to the original NBO program without confusing a sharper certificate, a cheaper              13

14   representation, and a better economic policy.                                               14

15     The numerical implementation starts from economic primitives. In the scalar               15

16   investment economy, it fits a fresh ReLU continuation backward against targets              16

17   using its own previously fitted future network. In the two-capital economy, it trains       17

18   hidden locations in a convex squared-ReLU continuation, with continuous simplex             18

19   actions and continuous uniform innovations. The second experiment includes                  19

20   a flexible nonnegative ridge dictionary as well as a quadratic projection. Each             20

21   comparator constructs its own future targets and uses the same final economic               21

22   criterion. Neither conventional values nor inherited neural weights are used as             22

23   labels for the neural services.                                                             23

24     We report the complete 150-service catalogue, not only successful candidates. In          24

25   the original capped scalar services the neural procedure reaches the all-state target       25

26   0.06 in all twelve cases and 0.04 in six, whereas the spline reaches both targets in        26

27   every case. In the coupled services the neural procedure reaches 0.25 in six cases          27

28   and 0.10 in five; the flexible ridge reaches both in all six. These statements describe     28

29   the original service, including its original verification cap.                              29

30     A subsequent diagnostic holds both the neural weights and the deployed policies           30

31   fixed. Refining only residual verification certifies all six previously unresolved scalar   31

32   policies at 0.04 and the previously unresolved coupled policy at 0.10. The original         32
     6

1    failures are not relabeled. The diagnostic shows that they do not, by themselves,         1

2    identify a failure to construct an accurate neural policy. It does not identify the       2

3    population success probability of the optimizer, establish a new end-to-end runtime,      3

4    or change any policy’s cost.                                                              4

5        The comparative findings are equally integral to the paper. The original scalar       5

6    signed policy comparisons frequently favor the spline. The new coupled direct             6

7    intervals all contain zero and therefore do not order the policies. The precision         7

8    controller genuinely escalates after rejected low-precision proposals, but its com- 8
9    plete recorded work does not improve on fixed binary64 in this experiment. These          9

10   findings distinguish the certified capability of the construction from a comparative      10

11   advantage that would require additional evidence.                                         11

12       The paper retains its original economic subject and full theory. The exposition now   12

13   follows the economic problem, the operator, the policy theorem, and the evidence. 13
14   The complete previous article, proof supplement, and economic applications are            14

15   materialized unchanged within the current source tree as preserved companions; the        15

16   preservation map records their exact identities. No historical theorem or application     16

17   is deleted to obtain a shorter active exposition. The present article and new technical   17

18   supplement state and prove the additional results used by the new evidence.               18

19                                                                                             19

20                                                                                             20
                                   1.1. Relation to the literature
21                                                                                             21

22       Neural Bellman Operators sit within approximate dynamic programming, fitted- 22
23   value methods, and policy iteration, not outside them. Regression-based contin- 23
24   uation methods and simulation-based dynamic programming supply important                  24

25   conventional benchmarks (Judd, 1998, Powell, 2011, Longstaff and Schwartz, 2001,          25

26   Ernst et al., 2005). Actor–critic and approximate policy-iteration methods separate       26

27   evaluation and improvement (Konda and Tsitsiklis, 1999, Scherrer et al., 2015); safe- 27
28   improvement arguments likewise distinguish a proposed action from a verified              28

29   gain (Kakade and Langford, 2002, Pirotta et al., 2013). We use these distinctions to      29

30   organize the economic object returned by the algorithm.                                   30

31       Neural methods for differential equations provide ways to approximate values          31

32   and their derivatives (Sirignano and Spiliopoulos, 2018, Han et al., 2018, Hornik         32
                                   NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                      7

1    et al., 1990). Such approximation does not on its own verify a returned dynamic                  1

2    policy or its numerical execution. Our certificates can be applied to neural or                  2

3    conventional continuations. The neural-specific construction lies in fresh own-future            3

4    training and, in the coupled experiment, learned hidden locations. Its comparative               4

5    value must be isolated against flexible conventional methods, not inferred from the              5

6    certificate’s validity.                                                                          6

7      The general economic formulation admits recursive utility (Epstein and Zin, 1989,              7

8    Duffie and Epstein, 1992). The compact-domain policy theorem uses monotonicity                   8

9    and cash invariance, while the original Gaussian entropic construction has separate              9

10   moment and spectral conditions. These are different bridges, not interchangeable                 10

11   assumptions. The direct paired-cost identity below applies to conditional expec- 11
12   tation; it is not asserted for a nonlinear certainty equivalent. The confidence step             12

13   uses the empirical Bernstein inequality of Maurer and Pontil (2009). Our additional              13

14   ingredients are the policy-specific telescoping score, its certificate-derived support,          14

15   and the numerical enclosure of the original continuous-law comparison.                           15

16                                                                                                    16

1         The applications companion retains the full boundary specifications, infinite- 1
2    horizon transversality conditions, utility-domain restrictions, temporal-self equa- 2
3    tions, and game conditions. A finite-domain residual calculation does not establish            3

4    any of these by itself. In a game, certifying a specified deviation problem is not the         4

5    same as establishing the equilibrium fixed point. In continuous time, the controlled           5

6    generator contains state derivatives; freezing a critic during an actor update does            6

7    not suppress legitimate derivatives of the represented state-dependent function.               7

29                                 2.2. An executable NBO service                                   29

30                                                                                                  30
          An implemented operator maps economic data and a current policy to
31                                                                                                  31

32                             b C , W ) or (unresolved, W, diagnostic),
                         ( fb, π,                                                                   32
                                       NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                    9

1    where C is an economic certificate and W records the work used. Its stages are target              1

2    construction, fitting, feasible action selection, independent certification, and durable           2

3    storage. Critic fitting freezes its future inputs; action selection freezes the resulting          3

4    critic. In the fresh backward construction, date t uses the already fitted fbt+1 , not an          4

5    externally solved optimal continuation. The terminal target is the primitive g.                    5

6      A finite optimizer schedule is an algorithm, not a convergence theorem. A service                6

7    that has not met its target at its cap records failure, with the complete cost of                  7

8    its attempts. The retained factor-training analysis provides a finite constructive                 8

9    theorem for a separate structured class. The nonlinear schedules below instead have                9

10   an explicit, auditable capped-service estimand.                                                    10

11     For a fixed catalogue K and target ε, define                                                     11

12                                                                                                      12
       Hm (k, ε) = 1{method m stores a valid certificate at ε within its cap on object k }.
13                                                                                                      13
                                                                                                  (4)
14                                                                                                      14
     The empirical object is the full array of Hm , costs to the first qualifying checkpoint,
15                                                                                                      15
     and capped costs for failures. Its arithmetic average is a catalogue fraction. A prob-
16                                                                                                      16
     ability over random initializations would require a specified law and a sampling
17                                                                                                      17
     design for that different estimand. A post-review refinement of a failed object’s
18                                                                                                      18
     verifier is a new diagnostic and does not change (4) for the original service.

7      The proof compares the true and fitted objective at the two selected actions and          7

8    couples the future states. The weighted finite-menu bound is Cauchy–Schwarz                 8

9    applied to ei − e j . Details are in the technical supplement. An additive continuation     9

10   error common to all actions is irrelevant to the decision. A finite menu, however,          10

11   does not certify a continuous action set merely because it includes both observed           11

12   actions.                                                                                    12

13     For recursive maps with sup-norm Lipschitz constants β k on a common invariant            13

14   utility domain, a one-step perturbation νk propagates as bk ≤ νk + β k bk+1 . This is       14

15   the recursive counterpart of (6). Its use requires the common domain; it cannot cross       15

16   a singular utility boundary by extending a local constant. These transport bounds           16

17   retain the original refresh logic: use an anchor while its economic allowance covers        17

18   approximation and future change, and recertify or recompute when that condition             18

19   is exhausted.                                                                               19

20     The theorem certifies a full adapted policy, not a finite action menu or a grid of                   20

21   initial states. With expected cost its proof is a Bellman telescoping argument; for a                  21

22   monotone cash-invariant recursive evaluation it is an order argument. The numeri- 22
23   cal experiment is not needed for its validity. Its numerical usefulness depends on                     23

24   producing small, valid enclosures.                                                                     24

     12

1      The original actor allowance is valid on the entire state domain, so a finer critic      1

2    verification mesh does not invalidate it. Conversely, replacing it by an allowance for     2

3    a newly optimized fine-grid actor would certify a different policy. The implemen- 3
4    tation records the original actor hash and explicitly discards any fine-grid action        4

5    proposals generated internally by the verifier. Those proposals’ work and transient        5

6    storage are still charged.                                                                 6

7      The corollary does not promise that arbitrary precision is attainable by mesh            7

8    refinement. Nodal residual variation, the retained actor allowance, and the terminal       8

9    enclosure can leave a positive floor. Nor is the corollary a claim that additional         9

10   verification improves welfare. It distinguishes the accuracy of a fixed economic           10

11   decision rule from the sharpness of our knowledge of that accuracy.                        11

13                     3.3. Construction beyond post-training verification                      13

14     The original trainable-factor construction remains a constructive part of NBO. 14
15   For a quadratic own-policy continuation x ′ Pt x + ct , a square-activation network        15

16   ∥Wt x ∥2 /d + cbt trains every entry of Wt . Its population derivative loss and gradient   16

17   are                                                                                        17

18                                                                                              18
                  Lt (W; P) = 14 ∥W ′ W − dP∥2F ,    ∇W Lt = W (W ′ W − dP).
19                                                                                              19

20   If σmin (W ) ≥ s > 0, then ∥W ′ W/d − P∥ F ≤ ∥∇W Lt ∥ F /(ds). Together with the full- 20
21   vector actor defect, this gives a policy-error account for a jointly trained hidden        21

22   factor. The own-policy coefficient is re-evaluated for the returned policy, not silently   22

23   borrowed from an earlier training policy.                                                  23

24     The primitive-budget analysis in the retained article chooses a feasibility witness,     24

25   coefficient envelopes, rank margins, and update budgets before training. In the            25

26   Gaussian entropic extension it also verifies positive exponential-moment margins. 26
27   Under its stated spectral hypotheses, a scaled orthogonal initialization and bounded       27

28   gradient steps give the finite training construction proved there. The finite matrix       28

29   and Gaussian formulas are not a nonlinear dimension-scaling experiment. They               29

30   also do not imply that Adam or L-BFGS has a deterministic residual cap for a               30

31   general squared-ReLU network. We preserve both results and keep the two logical            31

32   statements separate.                                                                       32
                                   NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                             13

1                4. FROM ECONOMIC PRIMITIVES TO NONLINEAR CERTIFICATES                        1

2                                                                                             2
                            4.1. Backward fitting and selected policies
3                                                                                             3
       For each method, initialize a fresh terminal fit from g. At date t, construct tar-
4                                                                                             4
     gets from Tt f t+1 using that method’s own already fitted future. Fit the current
5                                                                                             5
     continuation under the frozen schedule. Then propose feasible actions, enclose
6                                                                                             6
     the Bellman infimum and the selected-action gap, and validate residual coverage.
7                                                                                             7
     Store a checkpoint before reporting its first target crossing. Continue through the
8                                                                                             8
     declared ladder only as specified by the original study. The record includes all
9                                                                                             9
     attempted resolutions, unsuccessful target queries, target-generation work, and
10                                                                                            10
     storage operations.
11                                                                                            11
       The scalar network has 63 ReLU features. Its training uses 513 states, a 257-
12                                                                                            12
     point target action construction, 400 Adam steps at learning rate 5 × 10−5 , and a
13                                                                                            13
     final output fit. The scalar verifier uses its own finer action and state ladders. The
14                                                                                            14
     conventional spline is reconstructed from the same primitives with its own ladder
15                                                                                            15
     and targets. A conventional target solver is not used to label a neural continuation.
16                                                                                            16
       The coupled neural continuation is
17                                                                                            17

18                                             48                                             18
                      f t ( x ) = at + bt′ x + ∑ ctj (wtj′ x + vtj )2+ ,   ctj ≥ 0.    (11)
19                                                                                            19
                                              j =1
20                                                                                            20
     Hidden locations and weights are trained. Nonnegative coefficient initialization and
21                                                                                            21
     a finite L-BFGS schedule of 300 iterations act on fresh own-future targets. The ridge
22                                                                                            22
     comparator instead fixes a 96-feature squared-hinge dictionary and fits nonnegative
23                                                                                            23
     coefficients to its own targets; zero coefficients may be pruned. Its dictionary seed
24                                                                                            24
     is fixed by date, so repeated training-seed labels do not create independent random
25                                                                                            25
     ridge representations. A quadratic projection is retained as a restricted comparator,
26                                                                                            26
     not treated as the strongest conventional alternative.
27                                                                                            27

28                                                                                            28
                        4.2. All-state and continuous-action verification
29                                                                                            29

30     At each state node, outward arithmetic encloses the actual network value and           30

31   the action objective. In the coupled economy, convexity in actions and a positive        31

32   strong-convexity constant yield a continuous-simplex primal–dual gap. A projected        32
     14

1    optimizer merely proposes the action; its stopping flag is not the certificate. State       1

2    interpolation then extends the signed residual bounds off-grid. The nearest-node            2

3    deployed actor receives a separate, uniform allowance for using its node action at          3

4    the actual state.                                                                           4

5      Uniform innovations are integrated through positive-part moments, rather than             5

6    replaced by a sampled innovation law. The elementary identity for a uniform                 6

7    variable U on [−h, h] is                                                                    7

8                                                                                                8
                                          (y + h)3+ − (y − h)3+
9                          E( y + U ) + =
                                      2
                                                                ,      h > 0.                    9
                                                   6h
10                                                                                               10
     Successive integration gives the corresponding formula for independent coordi-
11                                                                                               11
     nates and affine neuron arguments; zero coefficients are handled by the lower-
12                                                                                               12
     dimensional formula. Interval evaluation encloses cancellation and rounding. The
13                                                                                               13
     supplement specifies the mesh and action bounds used in the inherited verifier.
14                                                                                               14
       The residual account distinguishes a nodal enclosure from state coverage, actor
15                                                                                               15
     error, and terminal error. A nodal enclosure contains the effects of representation,
16                                                                                               16
     fitting, Bellman optimization, and arithmetic; it is not an identified decomposition
17                                                                                               17
     into a pure approximation error and a pure optimizer error. In particular, sub-
18                                                                                               18
     tracting a training loss from the residual does not estimate either component. The
19                                                                                               19
     diagnostic reports every separately available allowance without counting nested
20                                                                                               20
     subcomponents twice.
21                                                                                               21

22                                                                                               22
                                 4.3. Work, dimension, and execution
23                                                                                               23
       For tensor state, action, and innovation covers of sizes Kx , Ka , m, a direct verifier
24                                                                                               24
     pays work of order
25                                                                                               25

26                       ∑ Kx Ka {m(CF + Pt+1 ) + Cρ } + ∑ Kx Pt + Wstorage,              (12)   26

27
                     t< T                                  t≤ T                                  27

28   where Pt counts network evaluation work. Actor storage is of order TKx d a . Analytic       28

29   integration replaces m by the work of the moment formula, not by zero. With                 29

30   tensor covers, Kx scales as h− dx
                                  x ; analogous factors arise for action and innovation          30

31   discretizations. A dimension-explicit certificate is therefore not a dimension-free         31

32   algorithm.                                                                                  32
                                       NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                              15

1      If a Lipschitz innovation approximation contributes at most ζ t to Qt , widening                            1

2    residual endpoints by ζ t and the actor allowance by 2ζ t adds 4 ∑t Bt ζ t to (8). This                       2

3    is a discretization account, not permission to replace a continuous law without                               3

4    charging its error. Independent policy queries may have their own scenario-tree or                            4

5    simulation cost and are recorded separately.                                                                  5

6      The complete service includes own-future targets, fitting, unsuccessful refine- 6
7    ment, action extraction, verification, applicable policy queries, serialization, and                          7

8    durable checkpoint storage. Initialization and outer-process time are retained sepa- 8
9    rately. New local diagnostic clocks are not added to old GitHub-hosted clocks to                              9

10   manufacture an end-to-end estimate. All reported arithmetic certificates concern                              10

11   stored binary coefficients with outward enclosures. The inherited execution account                           11

12   treats rounded state acquisition and transition/cost evaluation separately from                               12

13   ideal policy loss.                                                                                            13

       To evaluate the original continuous law, simulate uniform innovation bins and

14                                                                                                        14

15                                                                                                        15

16                                                                                                        16

17                                                                                                        17
     are bounded independent observations. Let A, D and s2A , s2D be their sample means
18                                                                                                        18

                  6. INVESTMENT ECONOMIES AND THE COMPLETE EVIDENCE
5                                                                                              5

6                                 6.1. Design and provenance                                   6

7                                                                                              7
       The original science design has twelve scalar neural services, twelve scalar
8                                                                                              8
     spline services, six coupled neural services, six coupled ridge services, six coupled
9                                                                                              9
     quadratic services, and 36 services for each of three precision rules. The successful
10                                                                                             10
     source-bound execution contains all 150 records. The original first run, with four
11                                                                                             11
     nonfinite-network exceptions, is retained. Its numerical inverse-softplus initializa-
12                                                                                             12
     tion was subsequently replaced by an algebraically equivalent stable expression;
13                                                                                             13
     economic primitives, seeds, targets, budgets, and comparators were unchanged.
14                                                                                             14
     We do not treat the failed first run as nonexistent or combine its clocks with the
15                                                                                             15
     successful run.
16                                                                                             16
       The R44 protocol was committed before the additional diagnostics were executed.
17                                                                                             17
     It is a post-review protocol, not an original preregistration. It selects the seven
18                                                                                             18
     unresolved tight-target neural objects by their already observed outcomes and
19                                                                                             19
     freezes their weights and policies. The paired comparison uses all six coupled pairs
20                                                                                             20
     and all four originally declared states. A source manifest binds the original inputs,
21                                                                                             21
     the diagnostic code, outcomes, and publication sources. Every original record and
22                                                                                             22
     all five original science-source hashes were replayed.
23                                                                                             23

24                                                                                             24

32   original investment section and applications.                                             32

25   nal attainment table or justify a comparison between a selectively refined neural          25

7                                                                                                7

8                                6.7. What the evidence identifies                               8

9      The evidence identifies a finite from-primitives nonlinear construction, all-state        9

10   certificates for its returned policies, a useful retained-policy verification refinement,   10

11   and direct economic comparisons under the declared state and innovation design. 11
12   It also identifies the continued strength of conventional comparators. It does not          12

13   identify a general neural speed advantage, a nonlinear high-dimensional frontier,           13

14   or a population probability of optimizer success.                                           14

15     An adaptive sparse-grid or adaptive-partition comparator has not been exe- 15
16   cuted for the original neural optimizers. Section 15 adds a separate error-driven           16

17   coordinate-FVI comparator and three- and four-dimensional stress catalogue for              17

18   the constructive backend; neither retroactively changes the optimizer study. Neither        18

19   has a nonlinear dimension frontier or repeated isolated timing of every original            19

20   scalar and coupled service. Section 7.3 adds a separate predeclared horizon/price           20

21   catalogue with repeated isolated timing for the deterministic constructive backend          21

22   and spline; it does not replace the original experiments. The retained dimension- 22
23   explicit analysis exposes the tensor factors those studies would have to confront,          23

24   rather than treating a two-state experiment as their substitute. The general eco- 24
25   nomic framework and conditional constructive results remain in force; the missing           25

26   experiments concern comparative evidence for broader implementation claims.                 26

27                                                                                               27

28
             7. A CONSTRUCTIVE NEURAL SERVICE WITH A PRIMITIVE ERROR BUDGET                      28

29     The policy theorem is deliberately independent of the representation of the con- 29
30   tinuation. A construction theorem must do more: it must produce a representation,           30

31   feasible actions, and verified residuals from economic primitives. This section             31

32   supplies such a theorem for a deterministic ReLU backend. It does not assume                32
                                    NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                     23

1    convergence of the nonlinear optimizers used in the earlier experiments. Its distin- 1
2    guishing property is that the continuation has a prescribed Lipschitz constant even               2

3    when its Bellman labels are inexact.                                                              3

4                                                                                                      4

5                     7.1. Primitive conditions and the neural architecture                            5

6                                                                                                      6
       Consider the compact Bellman economy of Section 2, with a common action set
7                                                                                                      7
     A ⊂ Ra and state set X ⊂ Rd . All distances in this section use the ℓ1 norm. The
8                                                                                                      8
     innovation law and the monotone, cash-invariant functional ρt do not depend
9                                                                                                      9
     on the state or action. The transition remains in X. Suppose known nonnegative
10                                                                                                     10
     constants satisfy, uniformly in the innovation,
11                                                                                                     11

12                                                                                                     12
                            |ct ( x, a) − ct (y, b)| ≤ Cx,t ∥ x − y∥1 + Ca,t ∥ a − b∥1 ,        (17)
13                                                                                                     13
                   ∥ Ft ( x, a, z) − Ft (y, b, z)∥1 ≤ Fx,t ∥ x − y∥1 + Fa,t ∥ a − b∥1 .         (18)
14                                                                                                     14

15                                                                                                     15
     The terminal cost is L g -Lipschitz. These assumptions are additional conditions
16                                                                                                     16
     on the constructive backend, not a replacement for the more general feasible cor-
17                                                                                                     17
     respondences in the original economic formulation. A state-dependent feasible
18                                                                                                     18
     correspondence requires its own feasible-cover and transport constants.
19                                                                                                     19
       We require an effective numerical description of the primitives: certified finite
20                                                                                                     20
     state and action covers can be obtained at every positive radius; their nodes are
21                                                                                                     21
     feasible; and a numerical evaluator can enclose each Bellman query to any requested
22                                                                                                     22
     positive tolerance in finite work. The last condition includes evaluation of ρt , not
23                                                                                                     23
     just evaluation of the network. For expectation with a compact innovation domain,
24                                                                                                     24
     known cell probabilities and a certified innovation cover provide one such evaluator.
25                                                                                                     25
     If Ft is Fz,t -Lipschitz in the innovation, replacing each cell by a representative at
26                                                                                                     26
     distance at most hz contributes at most β t Lt+1 Fz,t hz . Arithmetic, probability-mass
27                                                                                                     27
     approximation, and any remaining integration error must be added. The experiment
28                                                                                                     28
     below instead integrates a piecewise-linear network analytically under the original
29                                                                                                     29
     continuous uniform law.

       Set

1    Choose state nodes xt,i of covering radius ht and action nodes at,j of covering radius                     1

2    k t . Given the continuation already constructed at date t + 1, compute numbers qbt,ij                     2

3    satisfying                                                                                                 3

     Define yt,i = min j qbt,ij , choose a minimizing action index j(t, i ) with a fixed tie rule,
7                                                                                                               7

8                                                                                                               8

9                                                                                                               9
                                      f t ( x ) = min{yt,i + Lt ∥ x − xt,i ∥1 }.                         (21)
10                                                  i                                                           10

11   At date T, use labels within eT of g and the same formula with constant L g . At any                       11

12   other date, deploy the action at,j(t,i( x)) attached to a nearest state node, again with                   12

13   a fixed tie rule. The action is feasible by construction. No future optimal value or                       13

14   conventional comparator is used as a label.                                                                14

15     Formula (21) is an exact ReLU construction, not an appeal to a universal approxi- 15
16   mation theorem. Absolute values use |u| = max{u, 0} + max{−u, 0}; minima use                               16

17   min{u, v} = u − max{u − v, 0}. A balanced tree of these gates implements the finite                        17

18   minimum. With Kt state nodes, the resulting sparse affine–ReLU circuit has O(dKt )                         18

19   coefficients and O(1 + log Kt ) depth with unrestricted affine fan-in. Signed interme- 19
20   diate values can be carried by their positive and negative parts. The construction is                      20

21   related to the classical Lipschitz extension formula of McShane (1934); neither that                       21

22   formula nor its elementary ReLU realization is claimed as new. Here its role is to                         22

23   close an own-future dynamic construction, including the deployed actor and every                           23

24   numerical allowance.                                                                                       24

     errors. Its residual and its deployed actor satisfy

     For fixed nodes and Lt , changing every label by at most δ changes the represented function
6                                                                                                          6
     by at most δ in the uniform norm; it does not change the Lipschitz bound.

       P ROOF : Monotonicity and cash invariance imply nonexpansiveness in the
9                                                                                                          9
     uniform norm. Thus Qt ( f t+1 ; ·, a) and Tt f t+1 are Lt -Lipschitz, while the action
10
     modulus is Lta . At a node, minimization of the numerical queries gives −et ≤                         10

     yt,i − Tt f t+1 ( xt,i ) ≤ Lta k t + et . Each cone in (21) is Lt -Lipschitz, as is their finite min- 11
12                                                                                                         12
     imum. The lower residual bound follows from the triangle inequality for every
13                                                                                                         13
     cone; the upper bound uses a nearest node twice, once for the cone and once
14

16                                                                                                         16
     ment is identical without action minimization. Substitution of these widths and
17                                                                                                         17

21     C OROLLARY 6—Deterministic termination at an economic tolerance: Let ε > 0,                         21

22   H = ∑tT=0 Bt , and s = ε/H. At nonterminal dates choose                                               22

23                                                                                                         23
                             et ≤ s/8,       Lta k t ≤ s/8,      Lt ht ≤ s/16.                      (25)
24                                                                                                         24

25                                                                                                         25
     At the terminal date choose eT ≤ s/4 and L g h T ≤ s/4. Terms with zero coefficient impose
26                                                                                                         26
     no restriction. Then the finite constructive service returns a feasible neural policy with loss
27                                                                                                         27
     at most ε. No probability law over initializations and no optimizer stopping criterion is
28                                                                                                         28
     needed.
29                                                                                                         29

30     The result follows because each discounted bracket in (24) is at most s. Effec- 30
31   tive compactness and the numerical evaluator give finite work for these choices. 31
32   Arbitrarily small tolerances may require arbitrary precision; the corollary does                      32
     26

1    not promise termination in fixed binary64. A finite implementation ladder can           1

2    still return a recorded failure, even though the unbounded constructive algorithm       2

3    terminates under the stated hypotheses.                                                 3

4      The earlier scalar constructive result in the retained paper implemented piecewise- 4
5    linear interpolation as a ReLU network. Its sufficient allocation controlled a label- 5
6    error-to-mesh ratio in the propagated slope. Formula (21) works in arbitrary finite     6

7    state dimension and enforces the slope bound directly. In particular, label and         7

8    action allowances can be allocated at order ε without a separate slope-preservation     8

9    requirement that those allowances be quadratic in the state spacing. This is an         9

10   improvement of that sufficient proof budget, not a lower bound for all spline           10

11   methods or evidence that conventional Lipschitz projection is unavailable.              11

12                                                                                           12

13                               7.2. What the construction costs                            13

14     The construction does not eliminate state coverage. With Kt state nodes, Jt action    14

15   nodes, Mt innovation representatives, and network evaluation cost Pt+1 , direct         15

16   target formation requires                                                               16

17                                                                           !               17
                                                                     T
18                   ∑ Kt Jt { Mt (CF,t + Pt+1 ) + Cρ,t + Cc,t } + O ∑ dKt            (26)   18

19
                    t< T                                            t =0                     19

20   arithmetic operations under the indicated evaluation model. Cρ,t includes the finite- 20
21   risk functional and probability processing. Certified integration can replace the       21

22   innovation sum but its own cost must be charged. Bit lengths, adaptive arithmetic,      22

23   and nearest-node policy queries are additional explicit costs, not constants sup- 23
24   pressed by a dimension claim. A rectangular state cover with radius ht can require      24

25   order h− d                                                     −a
            t nodes; a rectangular action cover can require order k t actions. The theo- 25
26   rem is therefore a dimension-explicit construction, not an assertion that a nonlinear   26

27   high-dimensional frontier has been executed or that the curse of dimensionality has     27

28   been removed.                                                                           28

29     In one state dimension, the finite envelope can be compiled exactly into a            29

30   piecewise-linear representation. Two passes compute the greatest L-Lipschitz mino- 30
31   rant of the node labels. Between neighboring nodes the envelope is the minimum          31

32   of the two adjacent affine cones. At most one additional intersection is needed per     32
                                    NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                           27

1    interval. Rational knots and values permit exact antiderivatives; an interval evalu- 1
2    ator encloses their numerical evaluation. This compilation evaluates the defining       2

3    network exactly and does not substitute labels from a separately solved Bellman         3

4    problem. The spline comparator receives the same exact-integration acceleration, so     4

5    it is not charged for an artificial dense neural evaluation loop.                       5

8                                                                                            8
       The new experiment tests complete services, not only previously failed neural
9                                                                                            9
     candidates. It uses a further nonlinear investment laboratory with x ∈ [0, 1], a ∈
10                                                                                           10
     [0, 1/4], β = 15/16, and independent z uniform on [−1/32, 1/32]:
11                                                                                           11

12                                                                                           12
                                        1
                         F ( x, a, z) = 32 + 11     1
                                             16 x + 16 x (1 − x ) + a + z,            (27)
13                                                                                           13
                                                  2    2    4            2
14                        c p ( x, a) = ( x − 11                  3
                                              16 ) + pa + 4a + 2( 8 − x )+ ,          (28)   14
                                               11 2
15                             g( x ) = 2( x − 16 ) + 2( 38 − x )2+ .                 (29)   15

16                                                                                           16

17   The invariant domain follows from monotonicity in x, a, z and the two extreme           17

18   transitions 0 and 1. Valid primitive state moduli are Cx = 23/8, Fx = 3/4, and          18

19   L g = 17/4; action moduli are Ca = p/2 + 1/4 and Fa = 1. The downside term              19

20   penalizes a low capital stock, and the quartic investment cost discourages rapid        20

21   adjustment. This is a numerical economic laboratory, not an estimated or calibrated     21

22   quantitative exercise.                                                                  22

23     The complete design was fixed before execution: horizons {2, 4}, prices {1, 4},       23

24   two methods, three isolated repetitions, and the full common state/action ladder        24

25   N = A ∈ {16, 32, 64, 128, 256, 512}. The targets are 0.5, 0.25, and 0.125 in units of   25

26   discounted cost. These are policy-loss tolerances, not equivalence margins for a        26

27   neural-minus-spline contrast. Every rung restarts from terminal primitives and          27

28   constructs its own future backward. The neural method uses (21); the spline uses        28

29   linear interpolation of its own labels and propagates its actual exact slope bound. 29
30   Both evaluate the same continuous innovation law analytically and round labels to       30

31   dyadic multiples of 2−40 . All numerical query errors are outwardly enclosed and        31

32   enter the certificate.                                                                  32

                         8. NEURAL CONTINUATION WITH AN ACTION WITNESS
26                                                                                                           26

27     A constructed continuation contains information about the action that produced                        27

28   each Bellman label. Discarding that information and subsequently controlling an                         28

29   unrelated actor can charge the same approximation twice. We now retain the label’s                      29

30   feasible action through the minimum circuit. This changes the returned policy, not                      30

31   merely the description of an old certificate. The construction continues to use the                     31

32   original economic primitives and its own fitted future.                                                 32
     30

       For either the original nearest-node cone actor or the spline actor, let Ci be the

25   On the scalar domain, all terms are piecewise affine. Their maximum is attained                  25

26   among the continuation knots, state nodes and cell boundaries; both adjacent actors              26

27   are included at a boundary. The exact rational maximum supplies a valid all-state                27

28   allowance without an additional verification mesh. Both conventional interpolation               28

29   and the nearest-node neural ablation receive this improved account. Thus the                     29

30   experiment does not contrast the new bound only against a deliberately loose                     30

31   baseline. The old separated bound remains recorded, and the smaller of two bounds                31

32   for the same returned policy may be used.                                                        32

1                     8.4. Matched construction, policy and certificate frontiers                           1

2                                                                                                           2
       The following matched catalogue is the frozen R46 experiment. Its statements
3                                                                                                           3
     about direct policy cost refer to the evidence then available; Section 13 supplies
4                                                                                                           4
     the new actual-policy comparison, and Section 12 executes the state-dependent
5                                                                                                           5
     construction. The design was fixed in the repository before execution. It retains
6                                                                                                           6
     the nonlinear continuous-innovation economy (27), horizons 2, 4, prices 1, 4, and
7                                                                                                           7
     all six resolutions 16, 32, 64, 128, 256, 512. Three methods and three isolated timing
8                                                                                                           8
     repetitions produce 36 services and 216 rungs. Each rung restarts from primitives.
9                                                                                                           9
     The two cone methods have identical critics and node-action arrays; their deployed
10                                                                                                          10
     selectors differ. The spline constructs its own labels. All methods receive the same
11                                                                                                          11
     exact integration and outward numerical arithmetic.
12                                                                                                          12
       The declared targets are 1/4, 1/8 and 1/16. Every unsuccessful rung and every
13                                                                                                          13
     capped failure is retained. Internal prefix clocks include target construction, witness
14                                                                                                          14
     or nearest-cell compilation, certificates and durable checkpoints; process clocks
15                                                                                                          15
     additionally include imports and the common non-economic warm-up. Runs use
16                                                                                                          16
     one selected CPU and one numerical-library thread, with method order rotated
17                                                                                                          17
     across repetitions and economic cells. Frequency is not controlled. These are local
18                                                                                                          18
     measurements, not a hardware-independent work theorem. Primitive counts and
19                                                                                                          19
     rational storage are recorded separately from clocks, and are not called complete
20                                                                                                          20
     floating-point or bit-operation counts.

2    latter failures are all horizon-four services and are retained. The witness bound         2

3    is strictly tighter than the nearest-node neural ablation in all 24 distinct econ- 3
4    omy/resolution comparisons, and tighter than the strengthened spline in 23 of 24. 4
5    The exception is T = 4, p = 1, N = 16, where the witness and spline bounds are            5

6    approximately 2.06039 and 2.03515. The declared dyadic targets have the same first- 6
7    crossing resolution for all three methods in every economic cell. Thus the certificate    7

8    improvement does not yield an earlier first crossing on this particular ladder. The       8

9    spline is faster in all 30 common successful target/repetition clock comparisons; the     9

10   witness construction is faster than the nearest-node neural ablation in all 30. These     10

11   local clocks and tighter regret bounds establish neither policy-cost superiority nor      11

12   a general neural speed advantage. The comparisons isolate the effect of carrying          12

13   the action witness and of using a joint policy certificate. They do not reclassify        13

14   earlier capped services or claim that the new selector was previously deployed. 14
15   In particular, an improvement shared by the strengthened nearest-node ablation            15

16   cannot be attributed solely to witness selection. A tighter regret certificate does not   16

17   identify the sign of a direct policy-cost contrast. The existing direct expected-cost     17

18   intervals and adverse precision findings remain unchanged.                                18

                   9. STATE-DEPENDENT CONSTRAINTS AND FEASIBLE WITNESSES
21                                                                                             21

22     Investment opportunities often depend on current capital, collateral or remain- 22
23   ing capacity. The common-action construction above does not by itself authorize           23

24   transporting a node’s action to a state where that action is inadmissible. We now         24

25   incorporate a feasible repair into the same neural Bellman construction. The original     25

26   controlled economy, rather than a relaxed control problem, remains the comparison         26

27   object.                                                                                   27

       This result closes a constructive route for a class of endogenous constraints within
13                                                                                                      13
     the original model. It does not assume that all feasible correspondences have a
14
     finite Hausdorff modulus. With s = ε/ ∑tT=0 Bt , sufficient nonterminal allocations                14

16                                                                                                      16

17                                                                                                      17

18                                                                                                      18
     is available, the larger query allowance of Corollary 9 is again sufficient. Setting
19

21     9.0.0.1. A capacity-constrained investment rule. For two nonnegative state coor- 21
22   dinates on [0, 1]2 , let A( x ) = [0, ( x1 + x2 )/2]. This is a state-dependent capital- 22
23   capacity constraint with K A = 1/2 in the ℓ1 state norm. The repair Γi ( x ) =                     23

24   min{ ai , ( x1 + x2 )/2} is feasible, satisfies (37) with ζ = 0, and has an elementary             24

25   affine–ReLU representation for each fixed witness. Thus changing capacity changes                  25

26   the actor and the constructive state modulus through an explicit term D Q /2. The in- 26
27   dex selector remains a finite comparison procedure. The supplement provides exact                  27

28   rational checks for this example; these are checks of the theorem and admissibility                28

29   contract, not an additional multidimensional performance study.                                    29

30     Neither feasible-net construction nor repair is free. With Jt,i node actions, target- 30
31   query work is summed over ∑i Jt,i rather than a common Kt Jt . Each deployed query                 31

32   also incurs the selected repair’s work and storage. Under imperfect state acquisition,             32
                                      NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                 37

1    repair must be certified feasible at the true state, or throughout the acquisition set          1

2    for a robust implementation. An error allowance for the objective cannot substitute             2

3    for that feasibility condition.                                                                 3

                                                                              Lt be the repre- 8
9                                                                                                     9

10                                                                                                    10

                                                               Lt |}.                         (46)

14   addition. A minimum of the computed scores satisfies (42) with νt = 2τt , including              14

15   ties. The supplement states the representability and arithmetic conditions and                   15

16   proves the error account. The general theorem also permits other certified score                 16

17   enclosures.                                                                                      17

18     This implementation does not round a rational switchpoint and assume that its                  18

19   original cell is unchanged. For the scalar comparisons, all cells intersecting a state           19

20   enclosure are retained. For acquired constrained deployment, a numerical index has               20

21   the objective allowance (46) and its action is repaired against the entire acquisition           21

22   set. Both routes address discontinuity directly.                                                 22

24                            10.2. The identical non-neural envelope                                 24

25                                                                                                    25
       The envelope construction must be separated from the language used to encode it.
26                                                                                                    26
     The classical Lipschitz extension (McShane, 1934), a native minimum computation,
27                                                                                                    27
     and an exact ReLU realization can represent the same function.
28                                                                                                    28

29     P ROPOSITION 13—Representation control: Fix the node locations, labels, slopes,                29

30   original action witnesses, and selector tie rule. A conventional min-plus implementation         30

31   and the affine–ReLU implementation of the same envelope return the same mathematical             31

32   continuation and policy. They have the same exact policy certificate and direct policy cost. A   32
     40

1    balanced realization uses O(dK ) arithmetic and comparison gates for K cones and depth                 1

2    O(log K + log d). Merely changing the representation does not improve the covering or                  2

3    Bellman-query factors.                                                                                 3

4                                                                                                           4
          P ROOF : For every pair of real numbers, min{ a, b} = a − ( a − b)+ . Applying
5                                                                                                           5
     this identity through a balanced reduction tree proves equality of the continuation.
6                                                                                                           6
     Carrying the same attaining index, with the prescribed tie rule, proves equality
7                                                                                                           7
     of the action witness and repaired action. The certificates and costs concern these
8                                                                                                           8
     mathematical objects. The gate count includes the coordinate absolute values and
9                                                                                                           9
     their sums. The supplement separately bounds floating-point gate errors; exact
10                                                                                                          10
     equality is not asserted for unchecked rounded evaluations.                                Q.E.D.
11                                                                                                          11

12     We execute this non-neural control on all 24 distinct scalar witness checkpoints. 12
13   It shares the defining data rather than masquerading as an independently trained                       13

14   competitor. The native selector and its ReLU encoding are thus one construction                        14

15   with two representations. The learned hidden-location methods studied earlier                          15

16   are different candidate generators. Their assessment, and that of the deterministic                    16

17   backend, remains a comparison of actual policies and complete computational                            17

18   work.                                                                                                  18
