                 11. WITNESS-PRESERVING COMPILATION OF THE NEURAL OPERATOR
20                                                                                                          20
       The cost of a Bellman construction depends on how its continuation is evaluated.
21                                                                                                          21
     A literal evaluation of the cone network in Theorem 10 visits every state label at
22                                                                                                          22
     every action–innovation query. Repeating that scan is unnecessary on a tensor cover.
23                                                                                                          23
     The following result compiles the same continuation and original action witnesses;
24                                                                                                          24
     it neither changes the fitted function nor substitutes a newly optimized policy.
25                                                                                                          25
       Let X = ∏dj=1 { x j,0 , . . . , x j,n j }, with strictly increasing coordinates and S = ∏ j (n j +
26                                                                                                          26
     1). The rectangular domain is the product of the coordinate intervals. Index the
27                                                                                                          27
     nodes lexicographically. For arbitrary real labels yi and L ≥ 0, write
28                                                                                                          28

     Forward and backward relaxations along each coordinate line compute all pairs (zv , ov )
15                                                                                                   15
     using O(dS) additions and comparisons and O(S) stored values and indices. After cell
16                                                                                                   16
     location, a value or exact witness query uses at most 2d corner pairs and O(d2d ) arithmetic
17                                                                                                   17
     operations. A binary search locates the cell in O(∑ j log(n j + 1)) comparisons; a uniform
18                                                                                                   18
     grid permits direct location.

       When node coordinates, labels and L are rational, exact rational preprocessing and
20                                                                                                   20
     comparisons implement these identities, including zero slopes, inconsistent labels and ties.
21                                                                                                   21
     Applying the same repair map to the original returned action gives exactly the same feasible
22                                                                                                   22
     policy and policy cost as the uncompiled construction. The residual and acquisition bounds
23                                                                                                   23
     of Theorems 10 and 11 are unchanged.

25     P ROOF : The triangle inequality gives f ( x ) ≤ zv + L∥ x − v∥1 . Choose an attaining        25

26   original site xi . In each coordinate choose the corner coordinate lying between x j and        26

27   xi,j . The resulting v ∈ V (C ) satisfies ∥ x − xi ∥1 = ∥ x − v∥1 + ∥v − xi ∥1 , proving (49). 27
28   Choose now i = i∗ ( x ). At this corner an owner with a strictly smaller transformed            28

29   value would improve the objective at x; an owner with the same value and a                      29

30   smaller original index would contradict the prescribed tie rule. Hence ov = i∗ ( x ) for        30

31   a suitable corner. Every candidate in (50) is an original cone, so its lexicographic            31

32   minimum is the desired pair. Coordinatewise forward and backward minimizations                  32
     42

1    evaluate the separable ℓ1 transform; keeping original indices in each lexicographic        1

2    comparison preserves ties. The supplement gives the line invariant, operation              2

3    account and numerical enclosure details.                                         Q.E.D.    3

       The nodal distance transform is classical; the linear-time coordinate algorithm is
5                                                                                               5
     described by Felzenszwalb and Huttenlocher (2012). We use it for a different com-
6                                                                                               6
     putational obligation: continuous off-grid Bellman queries must retain the original
7                                                                                               7
     feasible action and the specified tie rule. Equation (49) alone does not authorize
8                                                                                               8
     using the action attached to v; it is (50) that licenses the policy. The result belongs
9                                                                                               9
     to the cone-based NBO construction, but an identical native min-plus implemen-
10                                                                                              10
     tation can use the same compiler. It does not establish a computational property
11                                                                                              11
     exclusive to a neural encoding. Proposition 13 remains an equality statement, not a
12                                                                                              12
     comparison between independently trained methods.

16     Let Jt,i denote the size of the feasible action net at state node i, and let Mt be the   16

17   number of integration points in a certified query. Put Qt = Mt ∑i Jt,i . For a tensor      17

18   implementation, the representation part of a backward construction changes from            18

19   O(∑t dSt+1 Qt ) to                                                                         19

                                                 d

                          t                          j

23   Primitive evaluation, feasible-net construction, integration, error accounting, actor      23

24   formation, repair and output are additional terms. Exact arithmetic has a bit cost;        24

25   the count in (51) is not a claim that rational comparisons have unit physical cost. 25
26   Our records therefore report coefficient bit lengths, storage and complete process         26

27   and CPU times alongside the component counts.                                              27

28     In the two-state investment study, S = ( N + 1)2 , J = N + 1 and M = N. At               28

29   fixed dimension and horizon, compiling removes the extra state-cover factor from           29

30   the dense representation workload: O( TN 6 ) becomes O( TN 4 ), plus the explicitly        30

31   charged setup and output. This does not remove the tensor cover, the action cover, or      31

32   the innovation factor. In dimension d, both S and the 2d corner factor remain visible. 32

     Let A be the acquisition coefficient in (59) and K = ∑t<T βt DtQ . With exact witness selec- 10

14   the constructed implemented policy has loss at most ε against the full-information optimum.      14

15   A zero coefficient imposes no corresponding precision restriction. An observation fee adds       15

16   its explicit discounted charge to this loss account.                                             16

17     For fixed primitive parameters and horizon, choosing the smallest sufficient dyadic            17

18   resolution gives N = O(ε−1 ). The compiled algebraic construction uses O( TN 4 ) operations,     18

19   versus O( TN 6 ) for the literal dense cone evaluation, and stores O( TN 2 ) labels, owners      19

20   and actions. Executing the full dyadic ladder to this resolution has the same orders, with all   20

21   preceding attempts charged. Numerical precision, coefficient bit lengths, uncertainty-box        21

22   work and physical output latency remain separate costs; these orders are not a precision-free    22

23   or machine-independent timing claim.                                                             23

25     P ROOF : The first two allocations make the right side of (54) at most ε/2. The                25

26   acquisition and quantization terms add at most another ε/2 by (59). Compilation                  26

27   preserves the selected policy, so it does not change this account. On the dyadic                 27

28   ladder, the sum of every positive power of the earlier resolutions is bounded by                 28

29   a constant times that power of its largest resolution. Summing the complete alge- 29
30   braic operation counts and retained arrays proves the resource assertions. Effective             30

31   certified queries at the prescribed accuracy, rather than unchecked fixed-precision              31

32   evaluations, are part of the hypotheses.                                              Q.E.D.     32

1      The corollary turns the compiler into a sufficient work-to-economic-accuracy             1

2    allocation. A finite experimental ladder can still end before the sufficient resolution;   2

3    its failure must remain a failure. Nor does more precise observation eliminate             3

4    construction error: the two portions of the budget are distinct.                           4

                     11.2. Certified numerical queries and selector ambiguity
7                                                                                               7
       The executable compiler computes (zv , ov ) in rational arithmetic. A value query
8                                                                                               8
     encloses each corner expression outward and takes the minima of lower and upper
9                                                                                               9
     endpoints. For a state box contained in one cell, the possible original actors are
10                                                                                              10
     among its corner owners; for a box crossing cells, the union of their corner owners
11                                                                                              11
     is used. An owner is discarded only when its lower objective enclosure exceeds
12                                                                                              12
     another candidate’s upper enclosure. Exact ambiguous point queries use rational
13                                                                                              13
     comparisons and the original lexicographic rule. Uncertain-state queries retain
14                                                                                              14
     the hull of all remaining actions and propagate that hull through repair and the
15                                                                                              15
     dynamics.
16                                                                                              16
       Thus a floating-point tie is not silently assigned to a convenient policy. A numeri-
17                                                                                              17
     cal implementation that deliberately returns a nonattaining index instead has to pay
18                                                                                              18
     the νt allowance in (42). The present frozen-object checks separate exact identities
19                                                                                              19
     from outward-rounding diagnostics. Overlapping machine intervals are useful
20                                                                                              20
     checks; the identity theorem, rather than that overlap, proves that the compiler
21                                                                                              21
     represents the original policy.
22                                                                                              22

                 12. AN ORACLE-EXPLICIT CONSTRAINED INVESTMENT ECONOMY
24                                                                                              24

25     The following evidence is the frozen R47 study. Its results and limitations are          25

26   retained; Section 15 gives the new execution and decision evidence. We now execute         26

27   the feasible-witness construction in a coupled two-state economy. Capital affects          27

28   both future production conditions and current investment capacity. This makes fea- 28
29   sible repair part of the economic policy rather than an algebraic example appended         29

30   to a common-action calculation.                                                            30

31     Let x ∈ [0, 1]2 , β = 15/16, and 0 ≤ a ≤ κ ( x ) = 1/8 + ( x   1 + x2 ) /16. A common    31

32   innovation z is continuously uniform on [−1/32, 1/32], with opposite exposures in          32
                                   NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                   45

1    the two transitions:                                                                           1

2                                                                                                   2

3                 F1 ( x, a, z) = 1/16 + x1 /2 + x2 /8 + x1 (1 − x2 )/16 + a/2 + z,          (52)   3

4                 F2 ( x, a, z) = 1/16 + x2 /2 + x1 /8 + x2 (1 − x1 )/16 + a/4 − z.                 4

5                                                                                                   5

6    Define the shortage and imbalance cost                                                         6

7                                                                                                   7

8
                            b( x ) = ( x1 − x2 )2 /4 + 2(1/2 − x1 − x2 )2+ .                        8

9                                                                                                   9
     Stage cost is ∑2j=1 ( x j − 5/8)2 + b( x ) + pa2 + 4a4 ; terminal cost doubles the squared-
10                                                                                                  10
     target terms and retains b. The predeclared cells have T ∈ {2, 3} and p ∈ {1, 4}.
11                                                                                                  11
     These are normalized theoretical economies, not estimated parameters or calibrated
12                                                                                                  12
     welfare magnitudes.
13                                                                                                  13

14                                                                                                  14
       P ROPOSITION 16—Finite primitives and continuous-law queries: The state domain
15                                                                                                  15
     is invariant under (52). In the ℓ1 state norm, valid primitive constants are
16                                                                                                  16

17   Cx = 13/4,     L g = 9/2,    Fx = 11/16,     Fa = 3/4,     Ca = p/2 + 1/4,     K A = 1/16.     17

18                                                                                           (53)   18

19   The uniform tensor state grid with N subdivisions has covering radius h = 1/N. At each         19

20   node, N + 1 equally spaced feasible actions have covering radius at most k = 1/(8N ). The      20

21   repair is min{ ai , κ ( x )} and has zero extra displacement allowance. If a continuation is   21

22   L-Lipschitz, M uniform midpoint cells enclose its original continuous-law expectation after    22

23   adding the deterministic remainder L/(32M) to the numerical integration error.                 23

24                                                                                                  24

25     P ROOF : The primitive formulas give F1 ∈ [1/32, 29/32] and F2 ∈ [1/32, 27/32]               25

26   by interval bounds. The sums of absolute derivatives in each state column are at               26

27   most 11/16, and the action derivative has ℓ1 norm 3/4. Differentiating the costs               27

28   on the two shortage regions gives (53). Capacity is 1/16-Lipschitz and at most                 28

29   1/4, proving the grid and repair assertions. Within a uniform shock cell, the mean             29

30   distance to its midpoint is 1/(64M). Since the transition has shock modulus two in             30

31   ℓ1 , the expectation error is at most L/(32M). Thus quadrature does not change the             31

32   innovation law to a discrete one.                                                   Q.E.D.     32
     46

1      The two generators are the feasible cone witness and conventional fitted-value                1

2    iteration in the tensor bilinear basis. Each works backward against its own fitted              2

3    future, uses the same feasible action net, and evaluates the same continuous law                3

4    with the preceding remainder. No optimal value or other method’s continuation is                4

5    supplied as a label. Rounded labels, their valid query errors, slopes, node actions             5

6    and all policy bounds are stored.                                                               6

7      The conventional actor selects a nearest state node and repairs its action at                 7

8    the current capacity. It receives the one-sided account rather than only the older              8

9    separated certificate. For the bilinear interpolant, the Bellman-residual enclosure is          9

10                                                                                                   10

11                          −et − Lt h ≤ f t − Tt f t+1 ≤ DtQ k + et + Lt h.                         11

12                                                                                                   12

13   The interpolation weights are nonnegative and reproduce coordinates. Their                      13

14   weighted mean ℓ1 distance to the surrounding vertices is at most 1/N, which                     14

15   proves the stated extension from the nodal enclosure.                                           15

16                                                                                                   16

17        L EMMA 17—Complete cell-corner verification: For the bilinear actor, the selected- 17
18   policy allowance is et + Dt , where                                                             18

19                                                                                                   19

20
                            Dt = max sup {yt,i + Lt ∥ x − xi ∥1 − f t ( x )}.                        20
                                    i   x ∈Ci
21                                                                                                   21

22   Here Ci is the closed nearest-node cell and both adjacent choices are admitted on a boundary.   22

23   It suffices to evaluate the at most nine vertices obtained by splitting each such cell along    23

24   its node coordinates. Outward endpoint evaluation supplies a valid upper bound on Dt            24

25   without an additional verification mesh.                                                        25

26                                                                                                   26

27        P ROOF : On each resulting subrectangle the distance term is affine and the                27

28   continuation is bilinear. An affine function minus a bilinear function attains its              28

29   maximum at a vertex: first maximize over one coordinate, then over the other. 29
30   The label’s feasible action, repaired at the queried state, has objective at most               30

31   yt,i + et + Lt ∥ x − xi ∥1 by feasible transport. Taking the complete maximum proves            31

32   the allowance.                                                                       Q.E.D.     32

1                         12.1. A cap obtained from economic accuracy                            1

2                                                                                                2
       In this class the previously abstract covering, integration and repair operations
3                                                                                                3
     are finite routines with explicit costs. Let ST = ∑t<T βt , a p = Cx + K A Ca and b =
4                                                                                                4
     β( Fx + K A Fa ) = 705/1024. Define
5                                                                                                5

6                       L = max{ L g , a p /(1 − b)},    D = Ca + βFa L.                         6

7                                                                                                7

8    The primitive recursion gives Lt ≤ L and DtQ ≤ D at every horizon. Suppose that, 8
9    apart from the quadrature remainder, each nonterminal numerical query has error             9

10   at most ξ and terminal labels have error at most ξ T . With M = N, Theorem 10               10

11   implies                                                                                     11

12                                                                                               12

13                          G N ≤ CT,p /N + 2ST ξ + 2β T ξ T ,                           (54)    13

14                        CT,p = ST { D/8 + 2L + βL/16} + 2β T L g .                             14

15                                                                                               15

16   Thus a dyadic N ≥ 2CT,p /ε and ξ, ξ T ≤ ε/[4(ST      + β T )] suffice for loss at most ε.   16

17   Acquisition and action quantization add the explicit terms of (45); these must              17

18   receive part of the same tolerance. Fixed binary64, a fixed label lattice and a finite      18

19   three-rung study are not asserted to achieve every positive ε.                              19

20     For this two-state, one-action, one-shock implementation, Bellman queries num- 20
21   ber T ( N + 1)3 . Each has N midpoint evaluations. Direct cone evaluation examines          21

22   ( N + 1)2 cones, giving O( TN 6 ) primitive arithmetic work. Bilinear future evalua-        22

23   tion uses a local patch, giving O( TN 4 ) work for that implementation. Both store          23

24   O( TN 2 ) critic labels and actions. The present batched program additionally holds         24

25   O( N 3 ) state–action arrays and O( BN 2 ) temporary cone entries for fixed batch size      25

26   B. Feasible acquisition and repair add their recorded deployment work, rather than          26

27   being included at zero cost.                                                                27

28     With pb -bit arithmetic and multiplication cost M( pb ), the corresponding arith- 28
29   metic upper bounds acquire a factor M( pb + T + log N ), with coefficient conversion,       29

30   comparison and serialization charged separately. The precision must also meet               30

31   the query enclosure requirement. These are algorithmic upper bounds, not lower              31

32   bounds for all conventional methods. Proposition 13 shows that the identical native         32
     48

1    envelope has the same leading factors as its ReLU realization. No representation                       1

2    label removes the tensor dependence.                                                                   2

                          13. POLICY COSTS AND ECONOMIC METHOD CHOICE                                         26

27     The following evidence is the frozen R47 study. Its results and limitations are                        27

28   retained; Section 14 gives the new execution and decision evidence. We next evaluate                     28

29   the actual witness policy, not its distance bound relative to an unknown optimum. 29
30   The policies are the frozen objects at every common first crossing in the original                       30

31   witness catalogue. There are ten such economic-cell and target combinations. Three                       31

32   timing repetitions of an identical checkpoint are not three independent policies.                        32
     50

1      The primary initial-state law is uniform on [0, 1]. The additional fixed states               1

2    1/8, 1/2 and 7/8 test particular economic starting positions. At each of these                  2

3    forty groups, the three pairwise contrasts concern witness, nearest-cone and spline             3

4    policies. A fixed design specifies 131,072 common paths, forty-bit innovation bins              4

5    and family error 0.01 for all 120 contrasts. The economic comparison uses the same              5

6    continuous innovations as the original economy.                                                 6

7      Let [zm , zm ] enclose g − f Tm and let the initial continuation difference have range        7

8    [ a0 , b0 ] over the chosen initial-state law. With the one-sided residuals of (30), the        8

9    score difference of two policies has deterministic support                                      9

10                                                                                                   10

11                          a = a0 + ∑       Bt (−um     n
                                                   t − d t ) + BT ( z m − z n ) ,            (55)    11
                                      t< T
12                                                                                                   12

13
                            b = b0 + ∑ Bt (dm     n
                                            t + u t ) + BT ( z m − z n ) .                           13
                                      t< T
14                                                                                                   14

15   For a common random initial state, one bounds the difference f 0m − f 0n , not the two          15

16   separate ranges. Its extrema are obtained exactly from the union of the rational                16

17   piecewise-affine knots. Conditional expectation and then integration over the initial           17

18   law extend Theorem 4 to this estimand.                                                          18

19     Every simulated bin generates lower and upper score endpoints. When a state                   19

20   enclosure meets a selector boundary, the calculation encloses all possible actors and           20

21   subsequent states. It neither drops the path nor substitutes an unverified midpoint             21

22   actor. The mean and variance computations have rational roundoff accounts. For                  22

23   the declared family, log(4 · 120/0.01) < 11; a finite positive Taylor sum for exp(11)           23

24   certifies this inequality. Using 11 in (15) is conservative and avoids an unchecked             24

25   logarithm evaluation. The supplement gives the complete endpoint construction                   25

26   and arithmetic audit.                                                                           26

27                                                                                                   27

28        T HEOREM 18—Simultaneous policy comparison and adoption: Conditional on the                28

29   frozen policies and the independent uniform-bin model, the endpoint intervals constructed       29

30   with (55) and (15) cover all declared expected policy-cost differences simultaneously with      30

31   probability at least 0.99. If an interval [ Lmn , Umn ] has Umn < 0, policy m has smaller       31

32   expected cost than n on this event. For a previously specified tolerance η, Umn ≤ η certifies   32
                                   NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                     51

1    noninferiority within that tolerance, whereas [ Lmn , Umn ] ⊂ [−η, η ] certifies two-sided       1

2    proximity.                                                                                       2

3      More generally, fix a reference policy b and known implementation charges k m . Suppose        3

4    simultaneous intervals [ Lm , Um ] enclose Jm − Jb for a finite candidate catalogue, including   4

5    [0, 0] for b. Select m
                          b to minimize Um + k m . On the coverage event,                             5

6                                                                                                     6

7
                             Jmb + k mb ≤ min{ Jm + k m + (Um − Lm )}.                        (56)    7
                                           m
8                                                                                                     8
     Thus an economic choice made after seeing all intervals retains its declared uncertainty
9                                                                                                     9
     account. The catalogue comparison is within one common economic task and initial-state
10                                                                                                    10
     law, not across different prices, horizons or evaluations.
11                                                                                                    11

12     P ROOF : The score identity identifies the expected difference. Its interval end- 12
13   points are bounded independent observations within each group. Apply the one- 13
14   sided empirical Bernstein inequality of Maurer and Pontil (2009) to each lower                   14

15   and upper endpoint and take a union bound over 240 tails. The numerical enclo- 15
16   sures only enlarge the valid intervals. Independence between different contrasts is              16

17   unnecessary. On the resulting event, Jmb − Jb ≤ Umb and Um ≤ Jm − Jb + (Um − Lm ). 17
18   Minimize the former upper bound plus the known charge to obtain (56).                 Q.E.D.     18

19                                                                                                    19

20     The study fixes η = 1/1024 in its normalized cost units before execution. It is                20

21   a stated decision tolerance, not an estimated welfare threshold. Zero remains the                21

22   superiority threshold. An interval merely intersecting zero meets neither criterion              22

23   automatically. The fixed pseudorandom implementation supplies repeatable inputs;                 23

24   it does not prove the independent sampling model. The theorem states that model                  24

25   explicitly. No claim about initialization risk or the distribution of newly trained              25

26   policies follows from these frozen-policy comparisons.                                           26

27     Every direct interval contains zero, so the experiment identifies no signed pol- 27
28   icy ranking. Every interval is also contained in the predeclared decision band. It               28

29   therefore certifies two-sided expected-cost proximity for the specified initial laws             29

30   at that tolerance, rather than inferring equivalence from a failure to reject equality. 30
31   The uniform initial-state law is a distributional conclusion, not an all-state assertion. 31
32   The individual initial states are separate declared estimands.                                   32

11                                                                                           11

12                                                                                           12

13                    13.2. Economic interpretation and the NBO program                      13

14                                                                                           14

15     The new evidence connects a feasible implementation to two distinct economic          15

16   statements. First, the acquisition-aware theorem bounds the loss of a policy that can   16

17   actually be executed under uncertain state measurement and capacity constraints. 17
18   Second, the direct experiment certifies proximity of the original scalar policy costs   18

19   within an explicitly chosen decision tolerance, so that a user of that catalogue may    19

20   consider implementation charges without treating separate regret bounds as values. 20
21   These statements are stronger than a certificate comparison alone.                      21

22     Neither statement implies that a neural representation is indispensable. The iden- 22
23   tical native envelope is a mathematical control, and the conventional fitted-value      23

24   and spline implementations remain strong alternatives. The constrained experiment       24

25   demonstrates two-state continuous-uncertainty construction and nontrivial repair,       25

26   not a high-dimensional scaling result or an estimated economic application. Its tight   26

27   targets remain unachieved. The direct cost experiment does not cover the new con- 27
28   strained policies or nonlinear recursive utility; its expectation-specific conclusion   28

29   is not transferred to those settings. The controlled-diffusion, recursive-preference,   29

30   endogenous-preference, temporal-self and game developments below retain their           30

31   own conditions and original role in the NBO program. Their preservation is not          31

32   counted as a new comparative execution.                                                 32
     54

1           14. PRICED OBSERVATION AND DIRECT CONSTRAINED-POLICY DECISIONS                            1

2
       A state-error radius is an economic input when acquiring more precise infor- 2
3                                                                                                     3
     mation is costly. We specify a finite-bit observation technology and a controller
4
     replacement charge. These are primitives of a theoretical decision problem in re- 4
5                                                                                                     5
     source units, not estimates from a calibrated economy. They permit a decision
6                                                                                                     6
     interpretation without treating an interval containing zero as equality or treating an
7                                                                                                     7
     all-state regret bound as a ranking of two policies.
8                                                                                                     8

9                                 14.1. Choosing the observation precision                            9

       On [0, 1]d , a b-bit sensor reports, in each coordinate, the containing dyadic interval
11                                                                                                    11
     and its midpoint. The right endpoint 1 belongs to the last interval. The resulting
12                                                                                                    12

                                                            −(b+1)

15   For At ( x ) = [0, κt ( x )], use the lowest capacity on the reported box, then round the        15

16   selected action downward to spacing ∆t . Feasibility holds throughout that box. In               16

17   the affine capacity economy this lower capacity is computed exactly from the lower               17

18   box coordinates. The acquisition repair therefore has χt = 0 in Corollary 12.                    18

19     Suppose the same b is chosen before date zero and each coordinate-bit costs λ > 0              19

20   per date. The observation fee is paid in the same additive resource units as the                 20

1      P ROPOSITION 19—Optimal precision for the certified economic account: On a                     1

2    declared integer range bmin ≤ b ≤ bmax , the smallest minimizer of the right side of (59) is     2

3    characterized by the neighboring inequalities                                                    3

4                                                                                                     4

5
                    C ≤ A2−b        if b > bmin ,    C ≥ A2−(b+1)      if b < bmax ,           (60)   5

6                                                                                                     6
     with the smallest minimizer selected when equality gives two. The criterion can be decided
7                                                                                                     7
     by rational comparisons, without an unverified logarithm. It minimizes a proved upper
8                                                                                                     8
     account; it need not minimize the unknown actual policy cost as a function of precision.
9                                                                                                     9

10     P ROOF : The difference between the accounts at b + 1 and b is C − A2−(b+1) ,                  10

11   which is nondecreasing in b. The two neighboring comparisons are therefore neces- 11
12   sary and sufficient, with the indicated endpoint convention. Equation (59) follows               12
                                acq
13   by substituting (58) and ζ t = 2KtA r (b) + ∆t into (45).                               Q.E.D.   13

14                                                                                                    14

15     This calculation provides an explicit information-price comparative static: an                 15

16   increase in λ weakly decreases the smallest minimizing precision, while an increase              16

17   in the sensitivity account A weakly increases it. The result concerns the guarantee,             17

18   not a fitted distribution of measurement errors. The executed curves use every                   18

19   b = 4, . . . , 16 and every λ ∈ {1/16384, 1/4096, 1/1024}. All curves and endpoint               19

20   choices are retained, rather than reporting only an attractive radius.                           20

21                                                                                                    21

22                               14.2. The actual constrained-policy cost                             22

       Fix the two-state witness and bilinear-FVI policies stored at the finest original
24                                                                                                    24
     R47 resolution, N = 16. In each economic cell, the two policies are evaluated with
25                                                                                                    25
     the same observation contract: exact state, six bits per coordinate, or ten bits per
26                                                                                                    26
     coordinate. The finite-bit policies use robust capacity repair and action spacing
27                                                                                                    27
     1/4096; the exact-state policies are unquantized. Thus there are three distinct pairs
28                                                                                                    28
     of implemented policies, not three observations of an assumed continuous actor.

14        T HEOREM 20—Interval simulation and replacement decisions: For each of K fixed                 14

15   policy-pair tasks, partition the underlying initial and innovation uniforms into equal bins         15

16   and draw independent uniform bin indices. Suppose a numerical path routine returns                  16

17   bounded random endpoints L j , Uj enclosing the exact path difference for every continuous          17

29   If replacing either installed controller costs k > 0 once at date zero, an interval strictly        29

30   inside (−k, k ) proves that neither replacement recoups that fee in expected resource cost for      30

31   that task. An interval with upper endpoint below zero ranks the witness policy below FVI; a         31

32   lower endpoint above zero ranks it above FVI. Containing zero does not establish equality.          32
                                   NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                              57

1      P ROOF : Conditional on a bin sequence, integrating the continuous uniform              1

2    variables gives a conditional path expectation between its enclosing endpoints. 2
3    Averaging over uniform bin indices reproduces the original continuous law, so             3

4    EL j ≤ Dµ,b ≤ EUj . Apply the empirical-Bernstein inequality of Maurer and Pontil         4

5    (2009) to the lower and upper endpoint samples, with a union bound across 2K              5

6    one-sided assertions; certified moment and arithmetic allowances enlarge, rather          6

7    than shrink, the intervals. Finally, switching from FVI to witness changes cost by        7

8    Dµ,b + k, while switching in the opposite direction changes it by k − Dµ,b . Both are     8

9    positive when | Dµ,b | < k.                                                     Q.E.D.    9

       The experiment fixes K = 48, n = 262144 and α = 0.01 before execution. An exact
12                                                                                             12
     rational exponential bound verifies that ℓ = 10 is sufficient. Forty-bit bins cover the
13
     continuous initial and innovation laws. The initial laws are independent uniform co- 13
14                                                                                             14
     ordinates and the three fixed vectors (1/8, 1/8), (1/2, 1/2) and (7/8, 7/8), crossed
15                                                                                             15
     with all four horizon–price cells and three sensors. A fixed pseudorandom stream
16                                                                                             16
     makes the records reproducible; it is not a proof that those indices are independent
17                                                                                             17
     random variables.
18                                                                                             18
       The replacement fee is k = 1/64, one quarter of the maximal quadratic investment
19                                                                                             19
     expenditure at p = 1. It is an explicit theoretical transaction charge. We do not
20
     calibrate it to welfare data, choose it after inspecting the intervals, or infer an all- 20
21                                                                                             21
     state ranking from the four initial laws. Reporting the zero threshold separately
22                                                                                             22
     keeps the decision statement distinct from a claim of neural superiority.

           15. COMPLETE COMPILED-POLICY SERVICES AND ECONOMIC COMPARISONS
25                                                                                             25

26     The new study separates three questions: the cost of representing a fixed NBO           26

27   policy, the complete cost of constructing and certifying a policy, and the actual cost    27

28   of using one controller rather than another. A representation benchmark cannot by         28

29   itself answer either of the latter questions. Conversely, a conventional comparator       29

30   receives the same innovation law, feasibility conditions and all-state comparison         30

31   principle, not a deliberately inefficient implementation chosen to favor the neural       31

32   operator.                                                                                 32
     58

1                    15.1. Design, stopping rules and conventional baselines                   1

2                                                                                              2

3      The design was fixed before the complete execution. The principal catalogue uses        3

4    the original constrained two-state economy of Section 12, horizons T = 2, 3, prices       4

5    p = 1, 4, and N = M ∈ {4, 8, 16, 32, 64}. The three methods are the compiled cone         5

6    witness, uniform tensor FVI and error-driven nonuniform tensor FVI. There are             6

7    three isolated process repetitions for each method and economic cell: 36 services and     7

8    180 rungs. Every rung starts from primitives and uses its own future continuation. 8
9    All rungs are executed, including those after a target crossing; first-target work        9

10   charges the complete prefix, including every prior failure.                               10

11     The conventional adaptive method forms an own-future pilot on a 5 × 5 grid,             11

12   uses its coordinatewise second differences to allocate nonuniform dyadic knots, and       12

13   certifies with the actual largest coordinate gaps. Its pilot, allocation and repeated     13

14   target evaluations are charged. The section on nonuniform fitted-value comparison         14

15   in the supplement specifies the fixed heuristic and proves the strengthened nearest- 15
16   node certificate on these unequal grids. It is a conventional adaptive-coordinate FVI     16

17   comparator, not a sparse-grid method or an unrestricted optimal adaptive partition. 17
18     All methods share outward arithmetic, feasible action fractions and midpoint            18

19   integration with its deterministic remainder under the continuous uniform law. 19
20   Each service includes a finite acquired-state deployment checksum and durable             20

21   checkpoint output. Complete prefix wall and CPU times, method-specific eval- 21
22   uation counts, coefficient bit lengths, stored actor scalars, checkpoint bytes and        22

23   peak resident memory are recorded. The internal clock excludes startup and the            23

24   common non-economic warm-up; a separate process clock includes them. No inde- 24
25   pendent cost comparison is hidden inside that boundary: its joint service is reported     25

26   separately below.                                                                         26

27     Against uniform FVI, compiled witness is earlier in 3 of 30 common successful           27

28   target/repetition comparisons, later in 27, and tied in 0. Against error-driven FVI,      28

29   compiled witness is earlier in 15 of 30 common successful target/repetition compar- 29
30   isons, later in 15, and tied in 0. These are observed complete-prefix wall times, not a   30

31   hardware-general speed law. The comparison uses the prospective targets rather            31

32   than retrospectively selecting a favorable breakpoint. The full positive-tolerance par- 32

25                                                                                                             25
                                    15.4. What changes in the evidence
26                                                                                                             26

27     The compiler theorem supplies a constructive work reduction for a defined NBO                           27

28   backend without changing its policy. The complete catalogue measures that backend                         28

29   against uniform and error-driven conventional FVI with all failed resolutions in- 29
30   cluded. Direct constrained costs and priced observation support explicit economic                         30

31   decisions for fixed policies under declared information contracts. These results                          31

32   extend the original program, rather than replace it with a different subject.                             32
     64

1      The historical adverse findings remain: the original nonlinear optimizer catalogue,     1

2    selected residual-only recertifications, unresolved neural–ridge signs, and mixed- 2
3    precision timing records are not reclassified. Identical native and ReLU envelopes        3

4    remain one mathematical construction. The new deterministic repetitions do not            4

5    estimate a population success probability for nonconvex optimization; the direct          5

6    confidence statements concern their fixed policies under the sampling model; the          6

7    stress clocks do not identify hardware-general scaling. The comparative claims            7

8    in this article are restricted to the objects actually proved or executed, while the      8

9    controlled-economy and application-specific theoretical scope remains intact.             9

10                                                                                             10
                        16. ECONOMIC APPLICATIONS AND CONCLUSION
11                                                                                             11

12     The original applications concern policy-dependent recursive utility, endogenous        12

13   preference specifications, temporal selves, controlled diffusion problems, and dy- 13
14   namic games. Their complete economic statements and proofs are retained without           14

15   alteration. The centered and transport bounds explain how an NBO can support              15

16   economic counterfactuals when changes in future primitives are controlled. The pol- 16
17   icy theorem explains what must be verified for the actual policy. Neither statement       17

18   supplies an equilibrium or transversality condition absent from the application.          18

19     The nonlinear investment evidence adds an executable link from primitives to            19

20   learned continuations and directly certified policies. Its retained-policy diagnostic     20

21   demonstrates that a failed tight certificate can reflect the resolution of verification   21

22   rather than the quality of the deployed policy. Its direct coupled comparison answers     22

23   a different question—the relative cost of two policies—without confusing separate         23

24   regret bounds with a method contrast.                                                     24

25     The acquired-state result makes that returned policy implementable under a              25

26   stated information and arithmetic contract. The constrained economy supplies finite       26

27   covering, integration and repair routines; the direct scalar experiment supplies          27

28   simultaneous tolerance-based cost comparisons and a catalogue-adoption rule. 28
29   Their distinct assumptions and work accounts remain visible.                              29

30     The compiler adds a policy-preserving computational step to this program. It            30

31   connects the explicit neural continuation to an executable representation whose           31

32   per-query work no longer scans the full state cover. The economic implementation          32
                                   NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                               65

1    adds two further choices: how precisely to acquire the state, and whether replacing       1

2    an installed controller pays for itself. The first is governed by a priced, certified in- 2
3    formation account; the second by direct expected costs with an explicit replacement       3

4    charge. They are not inferred by ranking regret bounds.                                   4

5      The empirical content is correspondingly separated. Frozen-object experiments           5

6    identify representation costs for the same policy; complete services compare fresh        6

7    own-future constructions against conventional FVI; direct intervals concern fixed         7

8    policies and declared initial laws. The higher-dimensional executions expose the          8

9    remaining tensor and innovation costs. They do not constitute a dimension-free            9

10   approximation theorem, a calibration to welfare data, or a reliability law for unre- 10
11   stricted neural training. Within these boundaries, the operator supplies a construc- 11
12   tive route from economic primitives through a feasible implemented policy to a            12

13   verified resource decision.                                                               13

14     Neural Bellman Operators thus organize construction, certification, economic            14

15   reuse, and resource accounting around the policy actually returned. Their scientific      15

16   assessment requires all of these objects. Flexible conventional methods remain            16

17   important competitors; numerical activation is not a work saving, and a fixed             17

18   catalogue is not a general reliability theorem. Theorem 5 and Corollary 6 now supply      18

19   a separate deterministic reliability statement for an explicitly constructed neural       19

20   backend. They do not retroactively prove convergence of the earlier optimizers. 20
21   Preserving those distinctions makes the original economic program testable while          21

22   retaining its full theory and applications.                                               22

23                                                                                             23

24                                                                                             24
                    APPENDIX A: R EADING AND PRESERVATION MAP
25                                                                                             25

26     The main article is the authoritative R48 exposition of the existing Neural Bellman     26

27   Operators paper. It includes the controlled-economy formulation, centered transport       27

28   and policy comparison, constructive continuation and original witnesses, acquired- 28
29   state feasibility, exact compilation, and the economic decision analysis. The technical   29

30   supplement supplies the proofs and numerical accounts needed for those results,           30

31   as well as every new direct interval and the complete first-target time partitions. 31
32   All R47 main and supplement labels, including their inherited results, remain in the      32
     66

1    corresponding active documents. The current response addresses the R47 report            1

2    item by item.                                                                            2

3      The experimental sections distinguish frozen historical evidence from new ex- 3
4    ecutions. Earlier optimizer, precision, scalar and coupled policy records are not        4

5    regenerated or relabeled. The full historical theory and application companions          5

6    are retained as ordinary files in the revision tree; preservation is not asserted to     6

7    be a new validation of every application. The main finite-horizon certificate needs      7

8    the monotonicity, cash-invariance, feasibility and comparison assumptions stated         8

9    here. The direct sampling result additionally uses conditional expectation and its       9

10   sampling model. Controlled diffusions, infinite horizons, recursive utility domains      10

11   and equilibrium applications retain their separate analytic conditions.                  11

12     The source tree contains the ordinary manuscript, proofs, bibliography, table          12

13   generator, scientific sources, complete frozen records and build instructions. A clean   13

14   rebuild audits the records and regenerates the documents without a new scientific        14

15   run or an expiring artifact download. The publication audit binds the review anchor,     15

16   scientific source, evidence, tests and compiled documents. These checks establish        16

17   a reproducible submitted object; they are not a substitute for mathematical peer         17

18   review.                                                                                  18

1                       APPENDIX: C URRENT R52 DEVELOPMENT                                     1

2                                                                                              2
                               APPENDIX B: I NTRODUCTION
3                                                                                              3

4      A continuation value is useful to an economist because it changes a decision. Its       4

5    prediction error is not the loss of the policy actually implemented. Nor is a smaller     5

6    upper bound on that loss evidence that one policy has lower cost than another. When       6

7    observation, action selection and verification are costly, the numerical procedure        7

8    and the economic decision have to be evaluated together.                                  8

9      We develop Neural Bellman Operators as a policy-evaluation and feasible- 9
10   improvement method. The economic object remains a controlled economy with                 10

11   policy-specific continuations. The operator constructs each date’s continuation us- 11
12   ing its own fitted future, returns a feasible policy, and accounts for its loss against   12

13   the Bellman optimum. A general signed policy sandwich permits arbitrary fitted            13

14   candidates. A constructive affine–ReLU backend additionally produces the candi- 14
15   date from primitive moduli, without assuming that a nonconvex training algorithm          15

16   eventually succeeds. Exact compilation retains the original action witness through        16

17   off-grid evaluation and state-dependent feasibility repair.                               17

18     The principal question in this revision is how that construction produces a cer- 18
19   tified economic answer with finite resources. State coverage, action search and           19

20   integration have different error coefficients. Tying them to one grid size conceals       20

21   the allocation problem. We derive a separated primitive budget and an optimal             21

22   continuous allocation for its leading work proxy, including lower resource bounds         22

23   and dyadic implementation. The empirical comparison then records the complete             23

24   positive-tolerance frontier of a fixed common resource ladder, not only an isolated       24

25   favorable target. All failed earlier attempts belong to the work required to return a     25

26   policy.                                                                                   26

27     The next question is what the returned policy does. We evaluate the actual policies     27

28   at the declared first crossings, even when two generators cross at different reso- 28
29   lutions. Independent interval simulation preserves the original continuous initial        29

30   and innovation laws and encloses discontinuous actors and robust capacity repair. 30
31   Conditioning on construction information permits simultaneous cost inference after        31

32   this selection. The result does not infer a policy ranking from two certificates and      32
     68

1    does not extrapolate three deterministic timing repetitions into an optimizer-success    1

2    probability.                                                                             2

3      Two economic decisions make this distinction operational. First, a controller          3

4    replacement is worthwhile only if its actual gain exceeds the replacement fee. We        4

5    identify the directional gain intervals and the full break-even fee account, retaining   5

6    but no longer relying on a single generous charge. Second, observation precision is      6

7    chosen on the same finite bit grid on which the implemented policies are evaluated. 7
8    A simultaneous upper-net-cost selector has a regret bound relative to the best           8

9    precision in that catalogue. The bound holds for every nonnegative information           9

10   price on one coverage event. This is a result about actual net policy cost, different    10

11   from minimizing a conservative all-state loss account.                                   11

12     These advances do not depend on calling a classical envelope neural. A native          12

13   min-plus envelope and its exactly equivalent ReLU circuit are one method with            13

14   two encodings. The original-owner compiler is available to both. We compare that         14

15   method with uniform multilinear fitted-value iteration (FVI) and two separately          15

16   recorded conventional refinement rules. The original curvature-bisection block           16

17   remained uniform; the later graded block was nonuniform. A new residual-driven           17

18   comparator below records its realized geometry and all pilot work. Each generates        18

19   its own future labels and receives the same feasible action menu, continuous-law         19

20   integration and one-sided economic criterion. The evidence must determine the cost       20

21   of the returned policy as well as certificate sharpness. No uniform neural dominance     21

22   is built into the design.                                                                22

23                                                                                            23

24        B.0.0.1. Related methods. Lipschitz extension supplies the cone construction (Mc- 24
25   Shane, 1934), and separable distance transforms supply its linear-work nodal engine      25

26   (Felzenszwalb and Huttenlocher, 2012). Approximation in min-plus spaces has an           26

27   established dynamic-programming literature: Lakshminarayanan and Bhatnagar               27

28   (2014) study min-plus projected Bellman equations and supremum-norm errors. 28
29   Bellman-inequality methods likewise construct lower value bounds and policies;           29

30   Wang et al. (2015) use iterated inequalities with linear or semidefinite optimization. 30
31   Our argument does not claim priority for these principles. Its subject is their con- 31
32   nection to an own-future feasible policy, original-owner continuous compilation,         32
                                        NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                    69

1    acquired-state implementation, complete resource allocation and direct economic                      1

2    comparison of the returned objects. The empirical-Bernstein component uses the                       2

3    bounded-sample inequality of Maurer and Pontil (2009); conditional selection and                     3

4    numerical path enclosures specify the estimand to which it is applied.                               4

5      The broader NBO program includes controlled diffusions, recursive utility, en- 5
6    dogenous preferences, temporal selves and games. Those results and their as- 6
7    sumptions are retained in the complete development edition and its full proof                        7

8    supplement, together with the original training, matrix-factor and precision experi- 8
9    ments. The present article supplies a self-contained constructive theorem–evidence                   9

10   chain. Its direct-cost statistical theorems concern expected discounted costs, not an                10

11   undeclared extension to every nonlinear recursive application. This organization                     11

12   preserves the paper’s economic subject while making the current contribution and                     12

13   the actual comparison burden explicit.                                                               13

14     The additional improvement result addresses the economically important case                        14

15   where the certified construction returns a more costly policy than a conventional                    15

16   competitor. We preserve that incumbent and permit only actions whose actual                          16

17   reference advantage is certified nonpositive after the numerical and observation                     17

18   allowances. At the final date of the original investment model, exact continuous- 18
19   law integration makes this gate executable without assuming a successful policy- 19
20   value fit. Both generators receive the same repair. The resulting experiment isolates                20

21   removable final-action loss from the policy gap that remains; it does not rename the                 21

22   compiler as a uniquely neural advantage.                                                             22

24        APPENDIX C: T HE ECONOMIC OPERATOR AND ITS POLICY ACCOUNT                                       24

25     Consider dates t = 0, . . . , T and an invariant compact state domain X ⊂ Rd . At                  25

26   date t, the action belongs to a nonempty compact set At ( x ). The transition is                     26

27   Ft ( x, a, Zt+1 ), the current cost is ct ( x, a), and the terminal cost is g( x ). Write B0 = 1     27

28   and Bt = ∏ j<t β j , with β j > 0. An implemented policy includes its observation rule,              28

29   action selection and feasible numerical realization, not just its stored parameters.                 29

30     For a continuation v, define                                                                       30

31                                                                                                        31
        Qt (v; x, a) = ct ( x, a) + β t ρt (v( Ft ( x, a, Z ))),   Tt v( x ) = inf Qt (v; x, a).   (65)
32                                                                           a∈ At ( x )                  32
     70

1    Throughout the deterministic comparison, the evaluations ρt are monotone and                 1

2    cash invariant on a common domain containing all functions and constant translates           2

3    used below. They are therefore nonexpansive in the supremum norm. Continuous                 3

4    objectives, effective compact feasible correspondences and measurable minimizers,            4

5    or measurable arbitrarily near-minimizers with their error charged, are assumed. 5
6    The stipulated conditional recursion gives Bellman comparison against all ad- 6
7    missible adapted controls. In the experiments ρt is conditional expectation, costs           7

8    are bounded and innovations have their original continuous uniform law. The                  8

9    direct-cost inference in Section J is specific to expectation; it is not automatically an    9

10   inference theorem for nonlinear recursive utility or games.                                  10

11     A Neural Bellman Operator is a construction and evaluation procedure for (65). 11
12   It forms targets using its own fitted f t+1 , returns a neural continuation and a feasible   12

13   policy, and records an economic error allowance. It does not receive the optimal             13

14   continuation as a training label. Arbitrary trained networks can be evaluated by the         14

15   first result below. The subsequent constructive backend specifies how a network              15

16   and its actor are obtained without assuming successful nonconvex optimization.               16

       A policy certificate answers a safety question relative to the optimum. It need
20                                                                                                  20
     not answer the comparison with an installed controller. We now make actual cost
21                                                                                                  21
     reduction a constraint on the improvement step. The continuation representation,
22                                                                                                  22
     including a neural realization, supplies proposals; a centered, policy-specific evalua-
23                                                                                                  23
     tion determines whether to implement them. The incumbent is always an admissible
24                                                                                                  24
     fallback.

17     The improvement principle is related to approximate policy iteration with safe- 17
18   guarded updates, including Metelli et al. (2021). We do not claim that monotone                             18

19   policy improvement itself is new. Here the relevant connection is between a signed                          19

20   own-policy evaluation account, action-centered numerical errors, original-owner                             20

21   construction, and the actual information-constrained action. The test is available                          21

22   to neural and conventional proposals alike. An exact native encoding of a ReLU                              22

23   continuation does not become a second independent method.                                                   23

17        C OROLLARY 26—Acquired-cell final improvement: In the declared investment                      17

18   economies, final conditional cost H ( x, a) = c( x, a) + βEg( F ( x, a, Z )) is strictly convex     18

19   in a, with second derivative at least 2p. Fix an observation cell C and the implemented             19

20   incumbent action aC . Choose a lattice minimizer aC∗ at the reported midpoint on the cell-wide      20

21   feasible set. Accept it only if an outward enclosure of H ( x, aC∗ ) − H ( x, aC ) is nonpositive   21

22   for all x ∈ C. Keep the incumbent on every other cell and at all earlier dates. The resulting       22

23   acquired policy weakly improves actual expected discounted cost for every initial state. This       23

24   guarantee does not compare it to a different full-information incumbent.                            24

25                                                                                                       25

26     The lattice is {k/4096 : k ∈ N0 }, truncated by the minimum capacity on the eight- 26
27   bit cell. Convex derivative bisection identifies the two neighboring lattice actions;               27

28   their actual cost contrast selects the midpoint proposal. The gate compares its cost                28

29   with the incumbent on the entire cell, not just at the midpoint. Exact rational point               29

30   comparison resolves a numerically ambiguous proposal tie. An uncertain true-state                   30

31   box may intersect several cells; the simulator retains their action hulls rather than               31

32   selecting a favorable cell.                                                                         32
                                      NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                             79

1      For each generator, old and repaired policies share their earlier trajectory. Their                       1

2    discounted cost difference therefore equals the conditional final contrast. Centering                       2

3    cancels state-only costs and tightens its interval enclosure. When two generators are                       3

4    compared, their trajectories differ and are simulated separately using common inno- 4
5    vations. Both comparisons concern actual policies; neither is obtained by subtracting                       5

6    two policy-loss certificates.                                                                               6

       The preceding improvement result compares an implemented policy with its
10                                                                                                               10
     own incumbent. We now connect that comparison to the original Bellman optimum.
11                                                                                                               11
     This distinction matters economically: a policy can improve strictly while remaining
12                                                                                                               12
     more costly than a conventional alternative. The result below preserves the original
13                                                                                                               13
     objective and gives explicit sufficient conditions for closing its optimality gap; it
14                                                                                                               14

31     The hypotheses separate three computational tasks. A trained continuation must                          31

32   be evaluated relative to the policy actually retained; the action search must ap- 32
                                     NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS                                    81

1    proximate the original feasible infimum; and the comparison must be valid over                    1

2    the available information set. A guarantee for only one of these tasks does not                   2

3    imply the theorem. The same conclusion applies to native and neural representa- 3
4    tions when they implement the same objects. In particular, it does not turn their                 4

5    common compilation saving into a neural-specific advantage. Safe policy improve- 5
6    ment is an established principle; the result here supplies the signed-width and                   6

7    acquisition-aware error account for the incumbent-preserving NBO construction.                    7

         The directed-cover assumption is substantive when constraints move with the
18                                                                                                     18

19                                                                                                     19

20                                                                                                     20

22       P ROPOSITION 29—Sharpness of the evaluation-width coefficient: Under the theo- 22
23   rem’s assumptions, the coefficient two on β t wtk+1 in (83) cannot be uniformly replaced by a     23

24   smaller constant, even for a one-period deterministic problem with two actions, an exact          24

25   greedy proposal, and exact contrast arithmetic.                                                   25

26                                                                                                     26

27       The theorem is constructive but conditional on verified error budgets. It does not            27

28   assert that a continuous neural fit uniformly approximates a discontinuous acquired- 28
29   policy value, or that refining a fixed acquisition contract eliminates its information            29

30   floor. The executed investment study below certifies a final-date improvement. It                 30

31   is not an execution of T full improvement passes, nor numerical evidence of the                   31

32   finite-sweep near-optimality conclusion. Exact finite-state regression tests exercise             32

4                APPENDIX G: P OLICY- RELEVANT CONTINUATION ERRORS                                          4

5      A continuation error matters for an investment decision through the actions it                       5

6    changes. The signed-width account above removes a datewise level error. It can nev- 6
7    ertheless charge for a nonconstant error that has the same conditional expectation                     7

8    under every feasible action. We now obtain a sharper account without changing the                      8

9    Bellman optimum, the acquisition contract, or the incumbent-preserving rule.                           9

       Write Pta f ( x ) = E[ f ( Xt+1 ) | x, a], and at pass k put etk = Jt − hkt . An action-contrast     10

11   certificate is a measurable nonnegative bound Rkt ( x; a, b) satisfying                                11

12                                                                                                          12

     selected proposal alone is sufficient for safety, but not for the accuracy conclusion,
17                                                                                                          17
